- keep **Feedback Loop Stack** responsible for end-to-end iteration review above build/debug/report evidence.

The worthy contribution here is a thin `cargo reportpack` / `cargo-report-pack/v0` layer with an explicit ranked `design/cargo-report-pilot-program.md` execution path: one that records what Cargo actually observed, which authority path actually imported it, what fallback order was attempted, what decoder/toolchain/schema family interpreted it, how local-private capture was projected into a portable/shared artifact, which bytes must remain `capture-only` or `quarantined-retained`, which fields were preserved / hashed / coarsened / omitted, when omitted or decoder-unknown fields are explicitly non-inferable rather than negative evidence, when any later summary/normalization must stay on a separate derived artifact, when the authoritative native basis later disappeared, what later packs are allowed to claim, when a retained report is only historical, when it is current only as-of a named observation epoch, when a later native or importer change forces re-import/re-review, and when stronger workspace/target/feature/toolchain/current-state sentences must fail closed instead of being inferred from one imported session or silently downgraded to projected or copied renderings. The next cut now also needs `fixtures/cargo-report-pack-kit/`, a formal `cargo-report-waiver-ledger/v0`, and the new `cargo-report-execution-receipt/v0` + `cargo-report-lineage-register/v0` so those semantics, temporary stronger exceptions, and production/derivation stories are frozen in schemas/examples/scenario cases and checked mechanically rather than drifting only through paragraphs.

## Current frontier correction (rev0301)

### Re-elevate: Device Lab Kit
Treat **Device Lab Kit** as one of the clearest near-term execution seams inside the broader **Testing Contract Stack** and beneath the archive’s **Firmware Productization Stack**.

Why elevate it again now:
- `probe-rs` now explicitly positions itself as a flexible embedded toolkit for running, flashing, debugging, RTT/`defmt`, many targets/probes, and even “awesome HITL setups”.
- `embedded-test` now documents a libtest-compatible host/target runner for ARM, RISC-V, and Xtensa, including per-test reset, async support, and explicit `probe-rs-tools` installation.
- The January 2026 `probe-rs` release moved `probe-rs`, `cargo embed`, and `cargo flash` binaries into a separate `probe-rs-tools` crate, which makes durable metadata above one install layout more valuable.
- The `defmt` book now says `defmt-test` belongs to the deprecated `probe-run` path and points users to `embedded-test`; `defmt` itself is now 1.0.
- Espressif’s `esp-hal` beta says hardware-in-the-loop testing was crucial for stabilization, while the Embedonomicon still treats flash algorithms, examples, templates, and debugging/testing guidance as essential target-enablement work.
- Archived quickstart/runner/tool repos (`cortex-m-quickstart`, `probe-run`, standalone `cargo-embed`) show the workflow surface is evolving rather than converging on one immortal template.
- The missing contribution is therefore **not** another board template, runner wrapper, or hosted board-farm service. It is the portable physical-target boundary above current tools and below testing/firmware/support/release consumers.

What to prefer next:
- prefer `gaps/embedded-device-labs-and-hardware-in-the-loop.md`, `design/device-lab-kit.md`, `design/device-lab-lane-map.md`, `design/device-lab-pilot-program.md`, and `proposals/epic-device-lab-kit.md` over another lab daemon, board-template empire, or one-runner wrapper;
- keep **Harness Protocol Kit** responsible for runner capability/discovery truth;
- keep **Test Execution Evidence Stack** responsible for generic run truth and runner-native recordings;
- keep **Firmware Productization Stack** responsible for the larger footprint/runtime/support/release composition;
- let **Device Lab Kit** own board/probe/fixture truth, lab capability/quarantine truth, run-plan truth, native capture imports, normalized on-device reports, and bounded downstream handoffs.

The worthy contribution here is a thin `cargo devicelab` / `lab-pack/v0` layer with an explicit `device-capture-import/v0`, not another embedded platform.

- Tarpaulin remains a real contrasting engine family, and its recent changelog now explicitly refuses meaningless delta coverage when run configuration changes would make comparisons dishonest.
- The missing contribution is therefore **not** another badge, dashboard, or hosted-service convention. It is the portable, criterion-aware evidence boundary above engines and below testing/safety consumers.

- Macro-improvements and the compiler-performance survey sharpen the same point from another direction: reducing proc-macro demand is explicitly good for build speed, dependency size, and debugging quality.
- The ecosystem already has several legitimate but non-equivalent lanes — runtime reflection, associated-const shape metadata, object-safe inspection, schema tracing, and proc-macro-heavy derives — so the missing contribution is a transition boundary above them, not a decree that one lane has already won.

- let **Reflection Transition Stack** own lane identity, comparison, migration posture, foreign-type/orphan-rule pressure, watch/wait outcomes, and bounded consumer handoffs.

- The accepted Cargo plumbing goal begins with **Locate project** and **Read the manifests for a workspace**, which means discovery is structurally earlier than governance, build planning, or artifact staging.
- Cargo 1.90 says the `cargo-plumbing` prototype still had to re-read manifests within commands because Cargo’s Rust APIs were not yet exposing the right structure cleanly.

The worthy contribution here is a thin `cargo discoverbound` / `discovery-pack/v0` layer, not another universal workspace tool.

## Current frontier correction (rev0287)
### Elevate: Starter Pack Kit
Treat **Starter Pack Kit** as a sharper missing execution bridge between **Adoption Decision / Ecosystem Atlas** and the real bootstrapping tools people actually use.

Why elevate it now:
- Rust’s December 2025 vision work says users need help navigating crates.io and do not have a clear place to get advice on a good “starter set” of crates.
- The 2025 State of Rust survey says online docs remain canonical while LLM/editor workflows are rising, which makes starter repos a machine-consumption surface too.
- `cargo new` is intentionally a simple template, but Cargo now also auto-inherits workspace fields during `cargo new` / `cargo init`, so starter repos increasingly encode workspace governance and org defaults from the beginning.
- `cargo-generate` proves template-driven bootstrapping is real, but its discovery model still points people at a GitHub topic rather than a shared provenance/refresh layer.
- The ecosystem now also has multiple domain-specific bootstrap paths — including `create-tauri-app`, `dx new`, `cargo lambda new`, and `esp-generate` — which is strong evidence that the missing layer is **above** generators, not another one of them.
- Rust’s 2026 flagship supply-chain work (public/private dependencies and SBOM support) means starter repos increasingly encode security/release posture on day one.

What to prefer next:
- prefer `gaps/starter-repos-template-provenance-and-freshness.md`, `design/starter-pack-kit.md`, `design/starter-pack-pilot-program.md`, and `proposals/epic-starter-pack-kit.md` over another framework quickstart wrapper, template-engine reinvention, or giant repo generator;
- keep **Adoption Decision / Ecosystem Atlas** responsible for lane choice and recommendation truth;
- keep **Workspace Environment Stack** responsible for environment realization truth;
- keep domain productization stacks responsible for their own runtime/package/support truths;
- let **Starter Pack Kit** own starter subject, imported sources, render-plan/adapter truth, rendered ownership, overlays, and freshness/refresh.

The worthy contribution here is a thin `cargo starter` / `starter-pack/v0` layer with explicit `starter-render-plan/v0` and `starter-render-report/v0` artifacts, not a new universal bootstrap command.

## Current frontier correction (rev0286)
### Elevate: Artifact Surface Kit
Treat **Artifact Surface Kit** as a missing lower-level anchor inside the current **Tooling Contract / Release Truth corridor**.

Why elevate it now:
- Cargo’s March 2026 build-dir-layout-v2 testing call says many projects still rely on unspecified build-dir details because the right Cargo features are missing.
- Cargo now publicly distinguishes final outputs in `target-dir` from intermediate build state in `build-dir`, and the compiler-layout docs say `artifact-dir` is public API while `build-dir` remains internal.
- Cargo’s unstable `--artifact-dir` feature exists because exact final filenames are otherwise awkward and JSON-heavy to recover.
- Cargo 1.93’s custom-final-artifacts discussion shows the ecosystem is moving toward explicit Cargo-mediated uplift rather than direct path spraying.
- Cargo’s external-tools surface still gives stable metadata and per-build JSON, but not a reusable final-output subject/handoff contract.
- `cargo-dist` proves strong downstream demand for machine-readable artifact/release manifests, but that is a release-oriented layer, not Cargo-native final-artifact truth.

What to prefer next:
- prefer `gaps/final-artifact-surface-identity-selection-outputs-and-handoffs.md`, `design/artifact-surface-kit.md`, and `proposals/epic-artifact-surface-kit.md` over more target-dir spelunking helpers, JSON-only wrappers, or release-only manifest work;
- keep **Build Cache Kit** responsible for intermediate state and reuse;
- keep **Build Extension Kit** responsible for authored/generated output declaration and uplift requests;
- keep **Release Truth Stack** responsible for source-package/release-bundle/signature/rebuild continuity;
- keep **Distribution Contract Stack** responsible for install/channel/fallback truth;
- let **Artifact Surface Kit** own final Cargo-facing output identity, path/origin truth, sidecar attachment, and bounded handoff.

The worthy contribution here is a thin `cargo artifacts` / `artifact-pack/v0` layer, not a replacement build system, release engine, or installer stack.

## Current frontier correction (rev0283)
### Elevate: Dataflow Surface Kit
Treat **Dataflow Surface Kit** as a genuinely under-modeled seam just below the existing event/data/runtime frontier band.

Why elevate it now:
- Rust now has several real but non-equivalent dataflow lanes: DataFusion is explicitly positioning itself for streaming applications; Arroyo makes event time, watermarks, state, and checkpoints first-class; Fluvio + Stateful Dataflows make connectors, SmartModules/Rust+WASM transforms, watermarks, grace periods, and idleness first-class; Timely keeps graph progress and replay first-class; and RisingWave keeps shared-source progress, backfill, barriers/checkpoints, materialized views, and job catalogs first-class.
- The missing contribution is therefore **not** another engine, dashboard, or connector marketplace. It is the portable review boundary above those lanes.
- The archive should now rank **source/sink truth + event-time/watermark/window/lateness semantics + state/checkpoint/backfill/recovery posture + materialization/freshness/progress evidence** as a cross-cutting need on the same level of seriousness as other recently-promoted productization/surface seams.

What to prefer next:
- prefer `design/dataflow-surface-kit.md`, `design/dataflow-surface-pilot-program.md`, `proposals/epic-dataflow-surface-kit.md`, and `gaps/streaming-dataflows-sources-time-state-materialization-and-progress-contracts.md` over another adjacent “streaming helper” idea;
- keep **Event Surface Kit** responsible for channel/event/replay truth;
- keep **Dataset Surface Kit** responsible for bounded dataset/publication truth;
- keep **Workflow Productization Stack** responsible for durable jobs/schedules/business-process truth;
- let **Dataflow Surface Kit** own continuous graph/time/state/materialization/progress truth.

The worthy contribution here is a thin `cargo dataflowcheck` / `dataflow-pack/v0` layer rolled out in honest pilots, not a winner-take-all runtime or another pile of engine-specific config helpers.

Current borrowing note: the archive should now treat **Trait Surface Kit + Pointer Surface Kit + Lending Surface Kit + Initialization Surface Kit** as a broader **Borrowing Frontier Stack**. Prefer **shared ranked pilots, explicit native-vs-adapter-vs-blocked posture, receiver/projection truth, borrowing-mode truth, staged-init/destruction truth, and migration-ready pack handoffs** over another smart-pointer manifesto, stream replacement pitch, trait macro empire, or constructor abstraction race.

1. **Policy Kit** (`cargo policy` + `policy-pack/v0`) — one explainable decision layer over imported evidence, explicit rule catalogs, waivers, and diffable outcomes across licenses, advisories, trust, lifecycle, inventory, signatures, support claims, and name-risk inputs instead of another opaque compliance score or shell-script gate; the next credible move is a ranked pilot program (package release gate → workspace split-scope → proc-macro/build-lane cooldown → artifact-linked release candidate → migration diff lane), not more bespoke CI wrappers or registry-health dashboards.
2. **Runtime Capability Kit** (`cargo capability` + `cap-pack/v0`) — one reviewable least-privilege boundary over filesystem/network/process/env/clock/host authority, scopes, delegation, analyzer-backed inference, and sandbox posture; the next credible move is a ranked pilot program (service inference → declaration/reconciliation → generated enforcement → framework attachment → policy/release consumer), not another sandbox DSL or auto-applied seccomp generator.
3. **Compile-Time Capabilities Kit** (`cargo ct` + `ct-pack/v0`) — one reviewable compile-time authority boundary for `build.rs`, native/wasm proc-macros, and `links` override lanes, with explicit unit inventory, execution lanes, declared capabilities, observed accesses, input-surface truth, determinism findings, waiver-aware policy, and named profile-fit reports instead of ambient compile-time trust.
4. **Pointer Surface Kit** (`cargo pointer` + `pointer-pack/v0`) — one reviewable boundary for pointer-like identity, alias/uniqueness truth, projection families, receiver/coercion posture, and weak/pin/drop semantics across ref-counted, pinned, foreign, intrusive, and custom pointer-like types; the next credible move is a ranked pilot program (shared ownership → pinned projection → foreign reference → receiver/dyn → transition/migration), with the shared-ownership lane now explicitly split by `design/pointer-shared-ownership-lane-map.md` into `Rc`, `Arc`, unique-to-shared, weakless/borrowed arc-family, publication-layer, and language-watch sublanes rather than one fake “Arc ergonomics” bucket.
5. **Public API Kit** (`cargo api` + `api-pack/v0`) — one reviewable release-admission boundary for public surface export, cross-crate provenance, declared-vs-inferred dependency exposure, semver reason codes, witness-based type-compatibility evidence, and MSRV verification across `cargo-public-api`, `cargo-semver-checks`, Cargo public/private dependencies, and future `cargo publish` gating; the next credible move is a ranked pilot program (single crate → workspace release group → downstream intake → policy correlation → change-impact consumer), not more isolated API diff tools.
6. **Compile Guidance Kit** (`cargo guidance` + `guidance-pack/v0`) — one reviewable boundary for crate-authored compile-time diagnostics, lint catalogs, fix-it guidance, example vectors, and extension-hook truth across proc-macros, lint packs, build integrations, and future compiler-extensibility lanes; the next credible move is a ranked pilot program (trait diagnostics → proc-macro UI/examples → lint catalogs → hook profiles → CI/editor consumers), not another isolated macro helper or stderr snapshot harness.
7. **DocProof Kit** (`cargo docproof` + `doc-pack/v0`) — one reviewable boundary for guide surfaces, executable examples, transcript teaching, hosted-doc assumptions, and attachable documentation evidence across rustdoc, docs.rs, mdBook, `trycmd`, and `trybuild`; the next credible move is a ranked pilot program (API docs + docs.rs → CLI transcripts → guide books → compile-fail teaching → release/support consumers), not another docs portal, prose-only style guide, or snapshot-only transcript harness.
8. **Validity Surface Kit** (`cargo validity` + `validity-pack/v0`) — one reviewable boundary for invalid values, niche/layout assumptions, ingress checks, unsafe-invariant truth, and attachable validity evidence across zero-copy, FFI, plugin/load-time, and unsafe abstraction lanes.
9. **Lending Surface Kit** (`cargo lending` + `lending-pack/v0`) — one reviewable boundary for borrow-friendly sequence surfaces, borrowing modes, async/runtime assumptions, adapter truth, and executable yield vectors across `Iterator`, streaming/lending iterators, `Stream`, nightly `AsyncIterator`, and future reborrow/generator lanes; the next credible move is a ranked pilot program (sync lending → async bridge → borrowing callback / async closure → pin/runtime lane → domain lane), not another attempt to crown one universal sequence facade too early.
10. **Initialization Surface Kit** (`cargo initsurf` + `init-pack/v0`) — one reviewable boundary for in-place construction, placement/address-stability, partial/fallible/async assembly, and guaranteed-drop/teardown truth across `MaybeUninit`, `Box::pin`, `Rc::new_cyclic`, nightly `UniqueArc`, `pin-init`, `moveit`, and self-referential helpers.
11. **Atomic Surface Kit** (`cargo atomicsurf` + `atomic-pack/v0`) — one reviewable boundary for memory-ordering contracts, target atomic-width/CAS posture, progress/reclamation truth, and checked shared-state evidence across std atomics, `portable-atomic`, `arc-swap`, `crossbeam-epoch`, Loom-tested lock-free lanes, and future kernel- or alternative-memory-model surfaces.
12. **Trait Surface Kit** (`cargo traitsurf` + `trait-pack/v0`) — one reviewable boundary for trait-family semantics, dyn posture, return-shape guarantees, impl coverage, and adapter/migration truth across async traits, RPITIT-heavy traits, split local/send families, and evolving supertrait/subtrait lanes.
13. **Encoding Surface Kit** (`cargo encodecheck` + `encoding-pack/v0`) — one reviewable boundary for type-model posture, wire-format semantics, borrow/zero-copy rules, canonicality/compatibility claims, streaming/buffer assumptions, and evidence across Serde-backed text/binary formats, protobuf/codegen lanes, archive/zero-copy stacks, and lighter specialized alternatives.

### Build performance + caching
- **Tier 0/1:** Build Cache Kit (`cargo cache` + `build-state-pack/v0`) — standardize build-state topology, cache-lane identity, lock-scope reports, cache-entry identity, reuse verdicts, and retention/exchange policy across **Cargo-native workspace-local state, editor-private duplication, Cargo-native user-wide intermediates, compiler-wrapper caches, container recipe/layer reuse, and CI/plugin exchange** instead of treating `target/`, wrappers, container layers, and remote restores as one opaque blob with folklore workarounds; read it as the first layer of the shared **Build-State Evidence Stack**, whose stack-level proposal candidate is now [`proposals/epic-build-state-evidence-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-build-state-evidence-stack.md).
- **Tier 0/1:** Change Impact Kit (`cargo impact` + `impact-pack/v0`) — standardize concrete change slices, semantic impact classifications, rebuild-scope reports, and relink-opportunity evidence across Cargo fingerprinting, `cargo report rebuilds`, public-API diffs, compile-time invalidation facts, and future relink-don’t-rebuild work instead of treating every rebuild as a binary cache miss or one opaque incremental-compilation quirk; read it as the middle layer of the shared **Build-State Evidence Stack** with Build Cache Kit and Build Doctor Kit.
- **Tier 0/1:** Build Doctor Kit (`cargo builddoctor` + `build-doctor-pack/v0`) — standardize workflow-aware build diagnoses, bottleneck classifications, and tradeoff-aware suggestions above Cargo report/build-analysis data, official build-performance guidance, and optional richer attachments instead of leaving slow-build triage to HTML timing pages, self-profile archaeology, and blog-post folklore; read it as the diagnosis layer of the shared **Build-State Evidence Stack** rather than a new build oracle.

### Supply chain + ecosystem governance
- **Tier 0/1:** Trust Signals Kit (`cargo trust` + `trust-pack/v0`) — standardize issuer-aware trust signals, scope-aware lockfile/workspace reports, freshness/cooldown facts, explicit trust-import profiles, artifact-recovery imports, and diffable trust packs across crates.io publisher controls, RustSec advisories, cargo-vet audits, cargo-deny graph-policy imports, lifecycle inputs, name-risk signals, and release evidence instead of another opaque crate score or a stealth second policy engine; treat [`design/trust-decision-lane-map.md`](./design/trust-decision-lane-map.md) as the rule that registry discovery, advisory feeds, audit attestations, graph-policy lint, artifact recovery, local decisions, and thin views must stay distinct, and [`design/trust-signals-pilot-program.md`](./design/trust-signals-pilot-program.md) as the ranked rollout plan so lockfile trust truth, audit/scope truth, publisher/freshness and graph-policy truth, artifact attachment, and thin registry/review consumers prove out before scoreboards or hard verdict UX.
- **Tier 0/1:** Trust Decision Stack (`cargo trust-decision` + `trust-decision-pack/v0`) — standardize the explainable handoff from trust evidence to local verdicts and bounded consumer views across registry discovery, RustSec advisories, cargo-vet attestations, cargo-deny policy-lint results, artifact-recovery imports, waivers, and diffable scope-sensitive decisions instead of another universal supply-chain dashboard or one hidden CI policy script; treat [`design/trust-decision-lane-map.md`](./design/trust-decision-lane-map.md) as the lane rule and [`design/trust-decision-pilot-program.md`](./design/trust-decision-pilot-program.md) as the ranked rollout plan so evidence-to-policy continuity proves out before registry/search/adoption summaries widen.
- **Tier 0/1:** Lifecycle Ledger Kit (`cargo lifecycle` + `lifecycle-pack/v0`) — standardize declared lifecycle intent, version-line support windows, verified successor pointers, explicit maintainer-help / handoff consent, visible maintenance reports, and derived lifecycle diagnostics so crates.io/Cargo/policy/trust tools stop overloading RustSec, README prose, yanks, or repo-activity heuristics as the lifecycle channel.
- **Tier 0/1:** Stewardship Ops Kit (`cargo steward` + `steward-pack/v0`) — standardize queue policy, queue snapshots, review-pressure reports, explicit help requests, mentoring lanes, stewardship action reports, and support-routing profiles so projects can publish operational reality for Atlas/lifecycle/trust/policy/release/support-program consumers instead of burying maintenance operations in ad hoc labels, dashboards, meeting notes, and social pleas; the next credible move should be a ranked pilot program rather than a generic maintainer-health platform.
- **Stack note:** treat **Lifecycle Ledger Kit + Stewardship Ops Kit** as a coupled **Maintenance Reality Stack**. Lifecycle Ledger owns declared support/deprecation/succession intent; Stewardship Ops owns live queue state, pressure, help requests, mentoring capacity, and action logs; Policy/Atlas/Release/Trust/support-program consumers should import these as distinct signals rather than flattening them into one health number. See [`design/maintenance-reality-stack.md`](./design/maintenance-reality-stack.md).
- **Tier 0/1:** Maintenance Reality Stack (`cargo maintenance-reality` + `maintenance-reality-pack/v0`) — standardize project-scoped maintenance briefs, lifecycle/stewardship imports, visibility-aware routing handoffs, transition reports, and bounded Atlas/adoption/release/policy/fund/keystone consumer packs so the ecosystem stops reconstructing maintainer reality from READMEs, yanks, issue labels, blog posts, and private memory. Treat this as the thin layer above Lifecycle Ledger and Stewardship Ops, not as a dashboard or health score; the concrete proposal shape is now in [`proposals/epic-maintenance-reality-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-maintenance-reality-stack.md).
- **Tier 0/1:** Keystone Stewardship Stack (`cargo keystone` + `keystone-pack/v0`) — standardize ecosystem-criticality reviews, institutional-support posture, continuity and obligation profiles, transition reports, and consumer-facing keystone packs for projects that have become foundational Rust infrastructure, so Atlas/adoption, support/funding, and enterprise consumers stop reconstructing “critical project” status from popularity, announcements, and private memory alone. Treat this as the layer above Maintenance Reality and Trust, not as a replacement for them, and execute it through the ranked rollout in [`design/keystone-stewardship-pilot-program.md`](./design/keystone-stewardship-pilot-program.md) instead of starting with badges, rankings, or a blessed-crates canon.
- **Stack note:** treat **Maintenance Reality Stack + Trust Decision Stack + selected Library Productization / Release Truth / Support Envelope imports** as a coupled **Keystone Stewardship Stack** for foundational infrastructure. Maintenance Reality owns declared lifecycle and observed stewardship operations; Trust owns release/provenance/advisory-facing inputs; product/release/support kits own what users consume; Keystone Stewardship owns criticality, institutional backing, continuity risk, obligation posture, and transition reports. See [`design/keystone-stewardship-stack.md`](./design/keystone-stewardship-stack.md).

- **Tier 0/1:** SBOM Evidence Kit (`cargo inventory` + `inventory-pack/v0`) — standardize scope-aware, artifact-linked, format-crosswalked dependency inventory across Cargo SBOM precursors, `cargo-auditable`, CycloneDX/SPDX export lanes, and binary/container recovery instead of treating one exported BOM document as the whole truth. Treat [`design/inventory-evidence-pilot-program.md`](./design/inventory-evidence-pilot-program.md) as the ranked rollout so inventory continuity becomes attachable across package admission, release, and installation without semantic collapse; the concrete stack-level proposal shape is now in [`proposals/epic-inventory-evidence-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-inventory-evidence-stack.md).

### Ecosystem quality + testing
- **Tier 0/1:** Harness Protocol Kit (`cargo harness` + `harness-pack/v0`) — standardize harness capability declarations, subject discovery, suite/case hierarchy, benchmark/doctest posture, and runner-adapter contracts across libtest JSON work, `cargo bench`, nextest consumers, nightly custom frameworks, and stable ecosystem harnesses instead of another runner or another hidden unstable interface; treat `design/harness-protocol-pilot-program.md` as the ranked rollout plan (libtest capability/discovery → nextest adapter → custom-framework opt-in → benchmark lane → doctest/IDE lane) so Rust gets a portable harness contract before execution/result packs grow into a fake mega-schema.
- **Tier 0/1:** Test Run Evidence Kit (`cargo testrun` + `test-pack/v0`) — standardize test subject ids, binary/test inventories, runner semantics, machine-readable run results, flake observations, and attachment indexes across libtest JSON, `cargo nextest`, JUnit/libtest-like outputs, Miri lanes, and custom harness imports instead of another test runner or another XML-only CI convention; treat `design/test-execution-pilot-program.md` as the ranked rollout plan (default-harness/nextest truth → config-bound CI → coverage+Miri attachments → replay/fuzz/mutation consumers → downstream/device-lab consumers) so Rust testing gets a portable execution boundary before more specialized tools widen.
- **Tier 0/1:** Benchmark Evidence Kit (`cargo benchpack` + `benchmark-pack/v0`) — standardize benchmark subject ids, lane/counter truth, baseline provenance, runner/import posture, and native result/attachment lineage across the explicit lane map in [`design/benchmark-evidence-lane-map.md`](./design/benchmark-evidence-lane-map.md): cargo-bench foundations, Criterion-native statistical baselines, Divan counter lanes, `cargo nextest bench` imports, Iai-Callgrind deterministic profiler lanes, and CodSpeed hosted adapters; treat [`design/benchmark-evidence-pilot-program.md`](./design/benchmark-evidence-pilot-program.md) as the ranked rollout plan (stable local harness pair → runner import → deterministic profiler lane → hosted adapter lane → Perf Labs handoff) so Rust gets a portable benchmark-native layer before broader performance policy widens.
- **Tier 1:** Downstream Testing Kit (`cargo downstream` + `downstream-report/v0`) — reverse-dependency CI made easy, with explainable diffs.

### Testing quality + CI evidence
- **Tier 0/1:** Coverage Evidence Kit (`cargo cov` + `coverage-pack/v0`) — declare criterion-aware coverage intent, import execution truth, merge results honestly, and attach diffable/gated coverage evidence to CI instead of badge-only workflows; treat `design/coverage-evidence-pilot-program.md` as the ranked rollout plan (LLVM baseline → nextest+doctest merge → external/FFI merge → Tarpaulin contrast → criterion-aware safety import) so Rust gets a portable coverage boundary before MC/DC-facing consumers widen.
- **Tier 1:** Config Set Kit (`cargo configset` + `config-pack/v0`) — standardize bounded feature/cfg/target/profile matrices with explicit selection rationale, repo-scope import, skipped-space reporting, and reusable config ids for CI/testing/coverage/fuzzing/verification instead of hand-maintained YAML and `cargo-hack` folklore.
- **Stack note:** treat **Harness Protocol Kit + Test Run Evidence Kit + Benchmark Evidence Kit + Config Set Kit + Coverage Evidence Kit + FuzzPack Kit + Replay Kit + Downstream Testing Kit + Device Lab Kit** as an emerging **Testing Contract Stack**. Harness Protocol owns capability/discovery/adapter truth; Test Run Evidence owns generic run profiles, result truth, flake observations, and attachment indexes; Benchmark Evidence owns benchmark-native subjects, explicit lane-map semantics, baseline provenance, runner imports, and result/attachment lineage; Config Set owns bounded matrix ids; Device Lab owns physical-target board/probe/fixture truth, lab capability/quarantine truth, native capture imports, and normalized on-device reports; the other kits remain specialized evidence or subject-selection consumers. The next shared execution moves are now explicit: first the ranked rollout in `design/test-execution-pilot-program.md`, then the benchmark-native rollout in `design/benchmark-evidence-pilot-program.md`, then the criterion-aware rollout in `design/coverage-evidence-pilot-program.md`, then the physical-target rollout in `design/device-lab-pilot-program.md`; the stack-level proposal candidate remains `proposals/epic-test-execution-evidence-stack.md`, so future revisions should prefer **harness/run/benchmark/config/attachment composition** over more runner-specific output wrappers, CI-only result folklore, or one-number coverage services.

### Supply chain + distribution
- **Tier 1:** Signed Binaries Kit (`binpack/v0` + `cargo binverify`) — standardize signature metadata, verification, and org policy for prebuilt installs as the signed-install attachment layer inside the emerging Release Truth Stack rather than another bespoke release-side metadata file.

### Release engineering + distribution
- **Tier 0/1:** Release Pipeline Kit (`cargo ship` + `release-pack/v0`) — define one portable release boundary that composes crate publishing, dist manifests, installers, signatures, SBOMs, provenance, and attached evidence packs across existing release tools; this now looks strong enough to promote because Rust has credible point tools but still lacks one reviewable release boundary tying source publish, built artifacts, signatures, and rebuild evidence together.
- **Tier 0/1:** Consumer Install Kit (`cargo installproof` + `install-pack/v0`) — standardize the **leaf consumer-install event** above current tools and below update/support consumers so Rust can explain what users or CI requested, what candidates were visible, why one path was chosen, what verification actually ran, what landed on disk, and what the installing tool claims to manage instead of leaving acquisition truth trapped in shell history, CI fallback wrappers, and tool-local receipts; treat [`design/consumer-install-kit.md`](./design/consumer-install-kit.md) as the thin leaf boundary and [`proposals/epic-consumer-install-kit.md`](./proposals/epic-consumer-install-kit.md) as the concrete product-direction candidate.

- **Stack note:** treat **Consumer Install Kit + Signed Binaries Kit + Airgap Kit + Release Truth imports** as the shared **Distribution Contract Stack**. Consumer Install owns install subjects, visible candidates, install policy/plan, receipts, and managed-content claims; Signed Binaries owns downloadable-artifact issuer and verification truth; Airgap owns restricted-network mirror topology and validation posture; Release Truth remains imported producer-side evidence; Update Continuity stays above the first-install event rather than being flattened into it. The next shared move is the ranked rollout in [`design/distribution-contract-pilot-program.md`](./design/distribution-contract-pilot-program.md), and the stack-level proposal remains [`proposals/epic-distribution-contract-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-distribution-contract-stack.md).

### Supply chain + independent verification
- **Tier 1:** Repro Build Kit (`cargo repro` + `repro-pack/v0`) — standardize **lane-aware** rebuild evidence across source-package reproducibility, final-artifact compare modes, provenance/SBOM attestation attachments, offline-verification bundles, and lifecycle posture instead of treating every release-side supply-chain signal as one fake reproducibility verdict; treat [`design/repro-build-pilot-program.md`](./design/repro-build-pilot-program.md) as the ranked rollout plan (package lane → pure-Rust artifact compare → native/toolchain lane → dist release-asset lane → attestation lane → offline/lifecycle lane) so Rust gets a portable rebuild boundary before release policy or trust layers widen.
- **Stack note:** treat **Publish Set Kit + Release Pipeline Kit + Signed Binaries Kit + Repro Build Kit** as a shared **Release Truth Stack**. Publish Set owns source-package publication subjects, packaged payload reports, publish-time checks/waivers, authority/registry-path facts, and upload/index receipts; Release Pipeline owns the broader release subject / intent / manifest / policy / `release-pack/v0`; Signed Binaries owns installable-artifact issuer and verification truth; Repro Build now explicitly owns **source-package reproducibility, final-artifact compare truth, provenance/SBOM attestation attachment posture, offline-verification bundles, and lifecycle notes** through [`design/repro-build-lane-map.md`](./design/repro-build-lane-map.md) and [`design/repro-build-pilot-program.md`](./design/repro-build-pilot-program.md); Inventory Evidence and Distribution Contract remain imported handoff layers rather than hidden sublayers. The next credible shared move is still a thin `cargo release-truth` / `release-truth-pack/v0` layer sketched in [`proposals/epic-release-truth-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-release-truth-stack.md). See [`design/release-truth-stack.md`](./design/release-truth-stack.md) and [`design/release-truth-pilot-program.md`](./design/release-truth-pilot-program.md).

### Offline / restricted-network operations
- **Tier 0/1:** Airgap Kit (`cargo airgap` + `airgap-pack/v0`) — make Rust bootstrapping, tool installs, mirror configuration, and offline validation boring and auditable across Cargo, rustup, vendor/local-registry flows, and existing mirror tools; treat [`design/airgap-pilot-program.md`](./design/airgap-pilot-program.md) as the ranked rollout plan so the archive proves workspace-build, developer-tool-install, rustup-mirror, secure-mirror-verification, and integrated restricted-network lanes separately before pretending “offline Rust” is one thing.

### Supply chain + registry hygiene
- **Tier 1:** Typosquat Guard Kit (`cargo guard` + `name-risk-report/v0`) — explainable warnings + policy gates against lookalike crate installs.

### Supply chain + policy
- **Tier 0/1:** Policy Kit (`cargo policy` + `policy-pack/v0`) — standardize `policy-subject` / `policy-input-catalog` / `policy-rule-catalog` / `policy-waiver` / `policy-decision-report` / `policy-diff-report` across cargo-deny, cargo-audit/rustsec, cargo-vet, Trust Signals, Lifecycle Ledger, SBOM Evidence, Signed Binaries, Support Envelope, and typosquat/name-risk inputs instead of leaving dependency and release governance trapped in bespoke CI glue, exit-code folklore, and opaque local exception lists. Treat [`design/policy-pilot-program.md`](./design/policy-pilot-program.md) as the ranked rollout plan so the kit proves itself on concrete decision points before widening into broader org or ecosystem governance views.

### Supply chain + incident response
- **Tier 1:** Incident Kit (`cargo incident` + `incident-pack/v0`) — drillable incident response: exposure analysis + mitigation + rebuild checklists + portable evidence.

### Provider and override points
- **Tier 1:** Override Surface Kit (`cargo overridecheck` + `override-pack/v0`) — standardize declared provider slots, installation/replacement/fallback semantics, scope/multiplicity, distributed registration posture, and checked override evidence across `#[panic_handler]`, `#[global_allocator]`, panic/alloc hooks, `log`/`tracing` globals and scoped defaults, `inventory`, `linkme`, and future externally implementable items rather than another service locator, singleton helper, or magical registry macro.

### Filesystem + path semantics
- **Tier 1:** Filesystem Surface Kit (`cargo fscheck` + `fs-pack/v0`) — standardize path posture, rooted-resolution and symlink/traversal policy, mutation/durability semantics, temp/staging behavior, watch/walk backend truth, and checked filesystem evidence across `std::fs`, nightly `std::fs::Dir`, `cap-std`, `cap-std-ext`, `camino`, `tempfile`, atomic-write helpers, `notify`, `walkdir`, and `ignore` rather than another helper facade, virtual filesystem, or fake universal path badge.

### Process orchestration + subprocess semantics
- **Tier 1:** Process Surface Kit (`cargo procsurf` + `process-pack/v0`) — standardize direct-exec versus shell posture, argv/env/cwd semantics, stdio and pipeline topology, supervision/group/session/job-object behavior, PTY/TTY interaction, and checked subprocess evidence across `std::process`, `std::io::pipe`, Tokio, `async-process`, `duct`, `xshell`, `portable-pty`, `command-group`, `process-wrap`, `shared_child`, and signal-handling adapters rather than another shell helper, task runner, or fake universal process badge.

### Concurrency + shared-state semantics
- **Tier 0/1:** Atomic Surface Kit (`cargo atomicsurf` + `atomic-pack/v0`) — standardize reviewable memory-ordering contracts, target atomic-width/CAS posture, progress/reclamation truth, shared-state shape, and checked evidence across std atomics, `portable-atomic`, read-mostly `Arc` publication, lock-free/reclamation-backed structures, and exceptional kernel/alternative-memory-model lanes instead of leaving atomic public support to unsafe comments, cfg folklore, and benchmark marketing.
- **Tier 1:** Synchronization Surface Kit (`cargo syncsurf` + `sync-pack/v0`) — standardize reviewable blocking/async coordination semantics, fairness, backpressure, lag/retention, clean shutdown, and verification truth across locks, channels, semaphores, notifications, and mixed-mode adapters instead of leaving public concurrency behavior to runtime docs, implementation details, and ad hoc test suites.

### Randomness + entropy semantics
- **Tier 1:** Randomness Surface Kit (`cargo rngsurf` + `randomness-pack/v0`) — standardize reviewable entropy-source posture, PRNG semantics, seeding/reproducibility, crypto-strength claims, target/backend truth, and checked evidence across `getrandom`, `rand`, portable named generators, lightweight alternatives, and crypto-facing RNG consumers instead of leaving randomness support to README prose, migration folklore, and fake “secure random” badges.

### Cryptography + provider semantics
- **Tier 1:** Cryptography Surface Kit (`cargo cryptosurf` + `crypto-pack/v0`) — standardize reviewable algorithm-family scope, provider/backend posture, key-material handling, side-channel/audit/compliance truth, and checked evidence across RustCrypto trait crates, `ring`, `aws-lc-rs`, `rustls` `CryptoProvider`, password-hash PHC lanes, secret-handling helpers, and remote-signer/HSM/KMS adapters rather than another algorithm implementation, blanket security badge, or fake universal crypto facade.

Cryptography Productization Stack = **Cryptography Surface Kit + Randomness Surface Kit + Runtime Settings Kit + Support Envelope Kit**. Ownership rule: Cryptography Surface owns algorithm/provider/key-material/side-channel/compliance truth, Randomness Surface owns entropy/seed/repro posture, Runtime Settings owns provider/feature/key-source/runtime-validation activation, and Support Envelope/DocProof own what is actually promised. The shared execution move is `design/cryptography-productization-pilot-program.md`, which ranks `rustls` provider/runtime-validation truth first, `aws-lc-rs` FIPS/non-FIPS build-and-pin truth second, RustCrypto generic-traits truth third, secret-handling/side-channel truth fourth, and downstream protocol/identity/service/support consumers later.

### Tooling substrate
- **Tier 0/1:** Compile Guidance Kit (`cargo guidance` + `guidance-pack/v0`) — standardize crate-authored compile-time diagnostics, lint catalogs, fix-it/help posture, compile-fail example catalogs, and extension-hook metadata across trait diagnostics, proc-macros, lint packs, build integrations, and future compiler-extensibility lanes instead of leaving supportive tooling to bespoke snapshots, issue comments, or one-off mini-compilers; the next credible move is a ranked pilot program (trait diagnostics → proc-macro UI/examples → lint catalogs → hook profiles → CI/editor consumers), not a universal extension manifest.
- **Tier 0/1:** Semantic Context Kit (`cargo semctx` + `semctx-pack/v0`) — standardize cross-crate semantic subject identity, exact resolution/build context, rustdoc JSON / docs.rs / `rmeta` / `rustc_public` input provenance, merge/completeness truth, and queryable semantic reports across semver tooling, docs/search frontends, fix/lint workflows, IDEs, and assistant consumers instead of making every tool rebuild its own partial workspace index; this now looks like one of the archive’s strongest tool-facing companion/evidence-substrate bets because docs remain canonical while machine consumers are rising, and the next credible move should be a ranked pilot program (local semver lane → docs.rs lookup lane → fix/lint handoff lane → editor/assistant slice lane → compiler-backed augmentation lane) rather than another universal workspace index or “AI context” blob.
- **Tier 1:** Cargo Report Kit (`timings-report/v0`, `rebuild-report/v0`, `report-pack/v0`) — standardize structured build reports for CI diffs and integration with caching/perf tooling; treat it as a key evidence import for the shared **Build-State Evidence Stack**, not the stack by itself.
- **Tier 0/1:** Build Interop Kit (`cargo interop` + `interop-pack/v0`) — unify machine-readable workspace graphs, build plans, execution events, and adapter posture for IDEs, wrappers, CI, and non-Cargo build systems; bridge Cargo plumbing, rust-analyzer discovery, and BSP-style integrations without forcing one build tool or pretending build-dir folklore is a contract.
- **Tier 0/1:** Manifest Surface Kit (`cargo manifest` + `manifest-pack/v0`) — standardize reviewable authored-manifest truth, packaged-manifest diffs, feature-surface metadata, and consumer-import reports across ordinary packages, workspaces, and embedded script frontmatter instead of leaving Cargo-manifest semantics to raw TOML diffs, registry pages, or tool-specific partial views. Treat [`design/manifest-truth-pilot-program.md`](./design/manifest-truth-pilot-program.md) as the ranked rollout plan (ordinary package → publish diff → workspace/discovery → script/frontmatter → consumer imports) so manifest truth becomes attachable before package-admission, migration, atlas, and support consumers widen.
- **Tier 0/1:** Publish Set Kit (`cargo publish-set` + `publish-pack/v0`) — standardize reviewable source-package publication subjects, packaged payload/file reports, publish-time check/waiver posture, authority/registry-path facts, and upload/index receipts across single-package and multi-package workspace publication instead of leaving source publication to manifest diffs, `cargo package --list`, CI workflow logs, and registry-page archaeology; treat [`design/publish-set-kit.md`](./design/publish-set-kit.md) as the base artifact contract, [`design/publish-set-lane-map.md`](./design/publish-set-lane-map.md) as the rule that single-package, workspace-set, alternative-registry, trusted-publishing, check/waiver, receipt/`pubtime`, and forensic lanes must stay distinct, and [`design/publish-set-pilot-program.md`](./design/publish-set-pilot-program.md) as the ranked rollout plan (single-package crates.io → workspace publish-set → alternative-registry/auth-required → trusted-publishing → check/waiver → receipt/`pubtime` → release/package-admission handoff) so source-publication truth becomes attachable before broader release and install consumers widen.
- **Tier 0/1:** MIR Analysis Kit (`cargo mir` + `mir-pack/v0`) — standardize semantics-aware MIR subject, capture-profile, capability/completeness, query/derived-graph, and diff/pack artifacts across `rustc_public`, `rustc_public_bridge`, `stable-mir-json`-style exporters, and downstream semver/safety/verification tooling instead of letting every compiler-aware tool invent a custom driver format and a different unspoken notion of comparability.

- **Stack note:** treat **MIR Analysis Kit + Lint Governance Stack + Compile Guidance Kit + Conformance Traceability Stack** as a coupled **Compiler Extensibility Stack**. MIR Analysis owns compiler-derived semantic export; Lint Governance owns selected policy, findings, debt, and fix handoff; Compile Guidance owns maintainer-authored diagnostics/help/examples and hook metadata; Conformance Traceability owns spec/assurance consumers. The next shared execution move is the ranked rollout in [`design/compiler-extensibility-pilot-program.md`](./design/compiler-extensibility-pilot-program.md), which should start with SemVer/compatibility lanes, then safety-critical lint lanes, then `rustc_public`/MIR analyzer lanes, then guidance/fix handoffs, and only after that widen to assurance/release/policy consumers. The proposal-layer candidate is now [`proposals/epic-compiler-extensibility-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-compiler-extensibility-stack.md): a thin `cargo toolcontract` / `tool-pack` composition layer rather than a universal plugin ABI or a Cargo-merger fantasy.

### Language foundations + library ergonomics
- **Tier 0/1:** Trait Surface Kit (`cargo traitsurf` + `trait-pack/v0`) — standardize reviewable trait-family semantics, dyn posture, return-shape guarantees, impl coverage, and adapter/migration truth across async traits, RPITIT-heavy traits, split local/send families, and evolving supertrait/subtrait lanes instead of leaving major trait design to proc-macro workarounds, RFC memory, and downstream folklore.
- **Tier 0/1:** Pointer Surface Kit (`cargo pointer` + `pointer-pack/v0`) — standardize reviewable pointer-like identity, alias/uniqueness evidence, projection families, receiver/coercion posture, and weak/pin/drop truth across ref-counted, pinned, foreign, intrusive, and custom pointer types instead of leaving the coming “Beyond the &” ecosystem to derive macros, unsafe comments, and folklore; treat `design/pointer-surface-pilot-program.md` as the ranked rollout plan (shared ownership → pinned projection → foreign reference → receiver/dyn → transition/migration), and treat `design/pointer-shared-ownership-lane-map.md` as the rule that shared ownership itself must stay split across `Rc`, `Arc`, unique-to-shared, weakless/borrowed arc-family, publication-layer, and language-watch sublanes before the language frontier fully settles.

- **Tier 0/1:** Initialization Surface Kit (`cargo initsurf` + `init-pack/v0`) — standardize reviewable placement/address-stability truth, staged/fallible/async assembly semantics, cyclic/self-reference exposure timing, and guaranteed-drop/teardown posture across `MaybeUninit`, out-pointers, `Box::pin`, `Rc::new_cyclic`, nightly `UniqueArc`, pin-init helpers, `moveit`, and self-referential helpers instead of leaving the coming in-place-init / immobile-types ecosystem to constructor docs, unsafe comments, and folklore.
- **Stack note:** treat **Trait Surface Kit + Pointer Surface Kit + Lending Surface Kit + Initialization Surface Kit** as a shared **Borrowing Frontier Stack**. Trait Surface owns dyn posture / return-shape / split-family truth; Pointer Surface owns identity / aliasing / projection / receiver truth; Lending Surface owns borrowing-mode / sequence-adapter / runtime truth; Initialization Surface owns placement / staged assembly / pin / teardown truth. The next shared execution move is now split into an explicit proposal layer plus the ranked rollout in [`design/borrowing-frontier-pilot-program.md`](./design/borrowing-frontier-pilot-program.md): [`proposals/epic-borrowing-frontier-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-borrowing-frontier-stack.md) should own the stack-level contract, while the pilot should still start with pointer/receiver truth, then lending/reborrow truth, then dyn-return plus initialization, then foreign/kernel lanes, and only after that widen to migration/atlas/support consumers.
- **Tier 1:** Allocation Surface Kit (`cargo allocsurf` + `allocation-pack/v0`) — standardize reviewable allocator / arena / pool semantics, OOM/failure posture, reset/drop truth, stable/nightly interop lanes, and checked resource evidence across process-global allocators, allocator-generic collections, `allocator-api2`, bump/arena crates, and future memory-resource surfaces instead of leaving public allocation behavior to README prose, nightly API archaeology, and benchmark folklore.
- **Tier 0/1:** Encoding Surface Kit (`cargo encodecheck` + `encoding-pack/v0`) — standardize reviewable type-model posture, wire-format semantics, borrow/zero-copy rules, canonicality/compatibility claims, streaming/buffer assumptions, and checked evidence across Serde-backed text/binary formats, protobuf/codegen lanes, archive/zero-copy stacks, and lighter specialized alternatives instead of leaving public encoding behavior to crate names, feature flags, derive macros, and fixture folklore.
- **Tier 0/1:** Time Surface Kit (`cargo timesurf` + `time-pack/v0`) — standardize reviewable clock-kind semantics, timestamp/civil/offset/zoned posture, timezone/DST handling, locale/calendar rendering, storage/wire mappings, and deterministic-test truth across `std::time`, Chrono, `time`, Jiff, ICU4X, SQL adapters, and future temporal lanes instead of leaving time behavior to crate choice, offset folklore, and DST bug archaeology; treat `design/time-surface-lane-map.md` as the rule that monotonic, wall-clock, civil/offset, zoned, localized, storage/migration, and fake-clock lanes must stay distinct, and treat `design/time-surface-pilot-program.md` as the ranked rollout plan (clock split → zoned/DST/tzdb-source → civil/wire → storage adapters → localization → fake clocks → migration).
- **Tier 0/1:** Vector Surface Kit (`cargo vectorsurf` + `vector-pack/v0`) — standardize reviewable lane catalogs, target-feature and dispatch/fallback posture, alignment/layout truth, operation-semantics caveats, and checked parity evidence across nightly `std::simd`, `std::arch` / `safe_arch`, runtime multiversioning, fallback-backed abstractions like `wide`, and coming scalable-vector/SVE lanes instead of leaving vector support to target flags, README caveats, and benchmark folklore; treat [`design/vector-surface-lane-map.md`](./design/vector-surface-lane-map.md) as the rule that portable-fixed-width, target-specific compile-time, runtime-multiversioned, fallback-backed, scalable-vector experimental, and adapter/import lanes must stay distinct, and treat [`design/vector-surface-pilot-program.md`](./design/vector-surface-pilot-program.md) as the ranked rollout plan (fallback-backed → target-specific compile-time → runtime multiversion → nightly portable SIMD → scalable-vector experiment → downstream consumer imports).

### Language foundations + compile-time surfaces
- **Tier 0/1:** Const Surface Kit (`cargo consteval` + `const-pack/v0`) — standardize reviewable const-evaluable API posture, parameter-model truth, evaluator-cost / code-size caveats, runtime fallback lanes, and channel/MSRV evidence across typenum bridges, const-generics-first APIs, fixed-capacity `no_std` structures, compile-time parsing/formatting utilities, and future reflection/comptime lanes instead of leaving compile-time support to README caveats, feature-gate notes, and compiler-error archaeology.

- **Tier 0/1:** Reflection Surface Kit (`cargo reflectsurf` + `reflect-pack/v0`) — standardize reviewable reflection acquisition lanes, type-shape metadata, value-access posture, registry/discovery semantics, and checked adapter evidence across future compile-time reflection, `bevy_reflect`, `facet`, `valuable`, `serde_reflection`, and inventory-backed registration instead of leaving cross-crate introspection to derive lock-in, hand-built registries, and ad hoc visitor APIs.

### Runtime coordination + synchronization
- **Tier 0/1:** Synchronization Surface Kit (`cargo syncsurf` + `sync-pack/v0`) — standardize reviewable blocking/async coordination semantics, fairness/starvation posture, backpressure and retention modes, close/drain/cancel behavior, and attachable interleaving evidence across std locks/channels, Tokio sync primitives, `parking_lot`, `crossbeam-channel`, `flume`, and mixed-mode adapters instead of leaving primitive selection to crate-name familiarity, scattered docs, and shutdown folklore.

### Language foundations + validity and unsafe boundaries
- **Tier 0/1:** Validity Surface Kit (`cargo validity` + `validity-pack/v0`) — standardize reviewable invalid-value classes, niche/layout assumptions, ingress-check posture, unsafe-invariant attachments, and attachable validity evidence across `MaybeUninit`-sensitive abstractions, `zerocopy`/`bytemuck`-style byte views, FFI/plugin/load-time boundaries, and future contract / checked-load lanes instead of leaving validity truth to derive docs, safety comments, and mixed-language folklore.

### Language foundations + toolchain assurance
- **Tier 0/1:** Acceptance Surface Kit (`cargo acceptsurf` + `acceptance-pack/v0`) — standardize solver- and borrow-check-sensitive pattern catalogs, compiler-lane profiles, workaround truth, and diffable acceptance reports across stable/beta/nightly, `-Znext-solver=globally`, Polonius, compiler UI-test practices, and library-side `trybuild` / `ui_test` harnesses instead of relying on scattered snapshots, issue links, and folklore-heavy “works on nightly” claims. - **Tier 0/1:** Spec Conformance Kit (`cargo conform` + `conformance-pack/v0`) — package spec references, executable vectors, capability declarations, and traceable conformance reports so Rust’s growing specification work turns into attachable toolchain evidence instead of remaining prose-only or internal-harness-only; treat it as the execution core of an emerging **Conformance Traceability Stack** with Acceptance Surface Kit and Safety-Critical Evidence Stack, and advance it through [`design/spec-conformance-pilot-program.md`](./design/spec-conformance-pilot-program.md) rather than as a standalone test-harness bid.

Support Envelope owns platform/runtime/docs lanes; Acceptance Surface owns compiler-lane/pattern acceptance; Debuggability exports debugger family/version/OS/visualizer truth; the shared execution move is the ranked rollout in [`design/compatibility-claims-pilot-program.md`](./design/compatibility-claims-pilot-program.md), now backed by [`proposals/epic-compatibility-claims-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-compatibility-claims-stack.md), which should start with released-binary support, then debugger tuples, then advanced-pattern acceptance, then mixed workspaces, and only after that widen into long-lived/safety-oriented consumers.

Compile Guidance owns maintainer-authored lint/diagnostic catalogs and examples; Lint Baseline owns selected policy, inherited/workspace posture, observed findings, debt baselines, and fix packs; Edit Workflow owns selection/application/verification of edits; Policy owns verdicts and waivers. ### Performance engineering + regression review
- **Tier 0/1:** Perf Labs (`cargo perf` + `perf-pack/v0`) — standardize reviewable performance subjects, measurement lanes, collector-configuration identity, baseline provenance, comparison verdicts, and diffable evidence packs across imported `benchmark-pack/v0` lanes, deterministic instruction/count lanes, rustc-perf-style compile scenarios, service-scenario workloads, and Cargo report imports instead of leaving regressions to screenshots, bespoke dashboards, and incomparable CI runs; treat it as the performance half of a broader **Resource Evidence Stack** with **Benchmark Evidence Kit + Footprint Kit**, and prefer ranked pilots centered on compile-workflow evidence, build-storage correlation, baseline hygiene, and reason-coded gates rather than a universal benchmark runner.

### Resource budgets + constrained deployment
- **Tier 0/1:** Footprint Kit (`cargo footprint` + `footprint-pack/v0`) — standardize explicit size/section/stack/allocation budgets, measurement coverage, and diffable resource evidence across native binaries, Wasm, embedded firmware, and allocator-specific profilers instead of leaving teams with per-tool output and CI folklore; treat it as the footprint half of the broader **Resource Evidence Stack** with Perf Labs, and prefer ranked pilots that start with compile/storage and release-artifact lanes before trying to universalize every resource workflow.

### Supply chain + compile-time execution
- **Tier 0/1:** Compile-Time Capabilities Kit (`cargo ct` + `ct-pack/v0`) — standardize compile-time unit inventory, execution lanes, declared capabilities, observed accesses, input-surface truth, determinism/cacheability findings, waiver-aware policy, and `ct-profile-catalog` / `ct-profile-fit-report` outputs across build scripts, native/wasm proc-macros, and `links` override lanes instead of another sandbox wrapper or one fake “safe build” badge.

### Least privilege + runtime authority
- **Tier 0/1:** Runtime Capability Kit (`cargo capability` + `cap-pack/v0`) — declare and diff runtime least-privilege posture across filesystem/network/process/env/clock/host authority, scopes, delegation, analyzer-backed inference, generated-enforcement candidates, and sandbox/permission layers; bridge `cap-std`, Wasmtime/WASI, Tauri capabilities, `cargo-capslock`, plugin/tool hosts, and org policy without inventing yet another sandbox. Treat [`design/runtime-capability-pilot-program.md`](./design/runtime-capability-pilot-program.md) as the ranked rollout plan so service inference, declaration-vs-inference reconciliation, seccomp/policy generation, framework attachment, and policy/release consumers prove out before the archive widens its claims.

### Build-time ergonomics + determinism
- **Tier 0/1:** Build Extension Kit (`cargo buildext` + `build-ext-pack/v0`) — declarative build steps, structured parameter passing, and safe final-artifact uplift that reduce opaque `build.rs` usage while preserving `OUT_DIR` isolation, collision reporting, and selected-package control; treat it as the structured-replacement pillar of the broader **Compile-Time Surface Stack**, and specifically as the main path toward the `declared-no-run` profile rather than as an isolated metabuild nicety.

### Scaling repos
- **Tier 0/1:** Repo Composition Stack (**Workspace Governance Kit + Config Set Kit + Build Interop Kit**) — near-term synthesis priority for Cargo-heavy revisions; make discovery roots, package-selection/default-members posture, inherited-vs-ignored settings, and bounded execution matrices compose across CI, editors, docs, and release lanes instead of leaving each consumer to infer “the repo” differently. Treat [`proposals/epic-repo-composition-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-repo-composition-stack.md) as the stack-level product direction: a thin `cargo repo-compose` / `repo-compose-pack/v0` layer with explicit consumer-lossiness notes rather than a monorepo manager or universal repo manifest.
- **Tier 0/1:** Tooling Contract Stack (**Repo Composition Stack + Build-State Evidence Stack**) — near-frontier machine-facing seam for Cargo-heavy workflows; make discovery/config truth, package selection, graph/plan exports, execution/build-state evidence, and downstream consumer imports compose across IDEs, CI, wrappers, and outer build systems instead of leaving tools to scrape `target/`, infer scope ad hoc, or tell incompatible stories about the same run. Treat [`proposals/epic-tooling-contract-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-tooling-contract-stack.md) as the current proposal-layer candidate: a thin `cargo tooling-contract` / `tooling-contract-pack/v0` direction with explicit stable-vs-experimental import posture and adapter-lossiness notes.
- **Tier 1:** Workspace Governance Kit (`cargo workspace doctor` + `workspace-report/v0`) — explain and diff workspace discovery, package-selection/default-members posture, and inheritance/overrides (nested workspaces, profiles, lints) to de-risk large repos.

### Registry + org UX
- **Tier 0/1:** Org Identity & Registry UX Kit (`cargo source` + `cargo publisher`) — standardize source/auth posture, publish-authority facts, project-family/namespace claim reports, and attachable publisher-source packs across crates.io Trusted Publishing, alternate registries, source replacement, and emerging namespace control instead of another config wrapper or one forced namespace scheme; treat [`design/publisher-source-identity-pilot-program.md`](./design/publisher-source-identity-pilot-program.md) as the ranked rollout and [`proposals/epic-publisher-source-identity-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-publisher-source-identity-stack.md) as the stack-level product direction so source truth, publisher authority, claim posture, package-admission handoff, and install/support consumers prove out before badges or policy engines.

### Cross-crate interop building blocks
- **Tier 0/1:** Interop Commons Kit (`cargo commons` + `commons-pack/v0`) — standardize when and how the ecosystem defines shared building blocks (`http`-style common types, Tower-style integration traits, honest adapter profiles, seam-readiness reports, conformance vectors, and adoption/stewardship metadata) instead of defaulting to either bespoke glue or premature mega-abstractions. Treat this as tightly coupled to Ecosystem Atlas rather than as an isolated abstractions project, and execute it through a ranked pilot program (HTTP request/response/body → service/layer middleware → one explicit watch/wait async borrowing/sequence seam) instead of jumping from schemas straight to pseudo-standardization.

### Ecosystem navigation + interop
- **Tier 0/1:** Ecosystem Atlas Kit (`cargo atlas` + `atlas-pack/v0`) — standardize curator-aware domain profiles, lane philosophies, stack-slot maps, explicit interop-seam references, selection evidence, freshness budgets, overlays, and diffable rendered guide / assistant artifacts across crates.io signals, docs.rs, trust/lifecycle/support inputs, and community curation rather than another ranking site or a single blessed-crates page; execute it as a ranked pilot program (CLI → services → client apps → safety-oriented lanes → Wasm/plugin lanes) and keep it coupled to Interop Commons so stack guidance points to real shared seams instead of folklore.
- **Stack note:** treat **Ecosystem Atlas Kit + Interop Commons Kit + Trust Decision Stack + Maintenance Reality Stack + Canonical Learning Stack + Semantic Context Kit** as a coupled **Adoption Decision Stack** for project-scoped selection work. Atlas/Commons own candidate lanes and seams; Trust Decision owns trust/policy/lifecycle evidence; Maintenance Reality owns sustainability posture; Canonical Learning owns docs/examples/guidance authority; Semantic Context owns local-fit and migration-sensitive grounding. The next credible shared move is the ranked rollout in [`design/adoption-decision-pilot-program.md`](./design/adoption-decision-pilot-program.md), now backed by [`proposals/epic-adoption-decision-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-adoption-decision-stack.md), which should start with greenfield CLI/internal-tool briefs, then service briefs, then safety-oriented lanes, then migration-sensitive existing-workspace briefs, and only after that widen to assistant consumers.

### Cross-language / native integration
- **Tier 0/1:** FFI Boundary Kit (`cargo ffi` + `ffi-pack/v0`) — make Rust↔C/C++ boundaries auditable, regenerable, diffable, and build-system-friendly; high leverage for adoption, safety, and large mixed-language codebases. Keep it as the boundary half of the explicit **Native Edge Stack**, not as a stand-alone generator bake-off.
- **Tier 0/1:** Native Dependency Kit (`cargo native` + `native-pack/v0`) — declarative provider-aware system/native library resolution, provider locks, and reviewable link plans that converge `system-deps`, `pkg-config`, `vcpkg`, `cmake`, vendoring, Cargo artifacts, and external-build-system overrides without flattening their differences; keep it as the provider/handoff half of the same explicit **Native Edge Stack**, not as another provider-specific helper crate or fake universal package manager.
- **Stack note:** treat **FFI Boundary Kit + Native Dependency Kit** as an explicit **Native Edge Stack**. FFI Boundary owns boundary manifests, generated artifacts, and drift; Native Dependency owns intent, provider locks, link plans, and reason-coded reports; the stack owns the joined review subject and external-build handoff. The next credible shared move is now backed by [`design/native-edge-stack.md`](./design/native-edge-stack.md), [`design/native-edge-pilot-program.md`](./design/native-edge-pilot-program.md), and the explicit proposal-layer candidate [`proposals/epic-native-edge-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-native-edge-stack.md): a thin `cargo native-edge` / `native-edge-pack/v0` layer rather than another binding generator, one-true C++ bridge, or universal native package manager.

### Wasm + plugin ecosystems
- **Tier 0/1:** Wasm Component Kit (`cargo wit` + `component-pack/v0`) — standardize `component-subject` / `wit-resolution-lock` / `component-surface-report` / `component-host-requirement-profile` / `composition-plan` / `composition-run-report` / `component-compat-report` / `component-publish-report` across plain `wasm32-wasip2` Cargo, `cargo-component`, `wit-bindgen`, `wkg`, `wasm-tools`, and Warg/OCI publication lanes instead of leaving teams to stitch together build logs, import folklore, and ad hoc package metadata. Treat this as a ranked transition program, not a one-shot schema dump: native Cargo export first, `cargo-component` bridge second, composition/host requirements third, registry/package identity fourth, and downstream consumer imports fifth.
- **Tier 1/2:** Plugin Surface Kit (`cargo plugincheck` + `plugin-pack/v0`) — standardize host extension-point identities, plugin manifests, capability handshakes, compatibility ranges, and checked host↔plugin evidence across native loading, stable-ABI crates, Extism, Wasm-based plugins, and framework plugin ecosystems instead of leaving extension support split across loader code, runtime-specific manifests, and release-note folklore.

### Platform support + delivery
- **Tier 0/1:** Compatibility Claims pilot program (`compat-pack/v0` importing `support-pack/v0` + `acceptance-pack/v0`) — use Support Envelope Kit and Acceptance Surface Kit together on a small ranked set of real pilots (released binaries, debugger lanes, advanced-pattern lanes, mixed workspaces, and long-lived support lanes) so compatibility claims become attachable evidence instead of prose. Treat this as the cross-stack umbrella in [`design/compatibility-claims-pilot-program.md`](./design/compatibility-claims-pilot-program.md), not as a substitute for lane-specific pilot discipline inside each half.
- **Tier 0/1:** Support Envelope Kit (`cargo support` + `support-pack/v0`) — standardize a reviewable support contract across dev-host, source-build, release-artifact, docs, and adjacent debugger-capability lanes, with explicit runtime-floor reports (OS/kernel/libc/SDK/ABI/CPU assumptions), observation evidence, drift diffs, waivers, and evidence policies so platform support stops living in README prose, ad hoc CI matrices, docs defaults, and linker folklore. Treat it as the platform/runtime half of the explicit **Compatibility Claims Stack** in [`design/compatibility-claims-stack.md`](./design/compatibility-claims-stack.md), and prefer a ranked **Support Envelope pilot program** (released binaries → source-build/custom-target → docs surface → runtime-floor derivation → long-lived support) over another platform badge or matrix generator.

### Cross-platform delivery
- **Tier 1:** Cross Toolchain Kit (`cargo crosskit` + `toolchain-pack/v0`) — unify provisioning of sysroots/C toolchains and emit explainable reports for cross builds (bridging cross/zigbuild/xwin).
- **Tier 0/1:** Sysroot Pack Kit (`cargo sysroot` + `sysroot-pack/v0`) — standardize reusable, explainable, and policyable custom standard-library builds across `build-std`, custom/tier-3 targets, hardened or instrumented stdlib profiles, and CI caches without baking one org-specific reuse scheme into Cargo; treat it as the stdlib-identity anchor of a broader **Toolchain Productization Stack** with Cross Toolchain Kit and Sanitizer Battery Kit.

Cross Toolchain owns compiler/linker/SDK/provisioning truth; Sysroot Pack owns rebuilt std/core identity and reuse compatibility; Sanitizer Battery owns runtime-analysis and instrumented-runtime truth. The next credible shared move is the ranked rollout in [`design/toolchain-productization-pilot-program.md`](./design/toolchain-productization-pilot-program.md), now sharpened by [`design/toolchain-productization-lane-map.md`](./design/toolchain-productization-lane-map.md), which should keep stock rustup, local build-std, reusable sysroot packs, compiler-pinned custom targets, activation/host-target split rules, instrumented/hardened runtime families, and external-build handoff distinct while it starts with instrumented stdlib, then hardened / ABI-modifying profiles, then custom-target / tier-3 activation, then shared-cache / CI reuse, and only after that widens to external-build-system, firmware, release, and safety consumers.

### Embedded + hardware reliability
- **Tier 0/1:** Device Lab Kit (`cargo devicelab` + `lab-pack/v0`) — standardize device profiles, lab capabilities, probe/flash/reset/log/test plans, native capture imports, and attachable on-device run reports across `probe-rs`, `embedded-test`, `defmt`, board templates, and vendor enablement flows so real-hardware CI stops depending on per-repo YAML folklore; treat `design/device-lab-pilot-program.md` as the ranked rollout plan so smoke/attach lanes, per-test-reset lanes, mixed transport imports, shared-lab/quarantine flows, and support/release consumers stop getting blurred together.
- **Stack note:** treat **Device Lab Kit + Toolchain Productization Stack + Footprint Kit + Observability Kit + Support Envelope Kit + Release Truth Stack** as a shared **Firmware Productization Stack**. Device Lab owns board/probe/flash/reset/run truth; Toolchain Productization owns target/linker/sysroot/stdlib activation; Footprint owns RAM/FLASH/stack/layout budgets; Observability owns logs/fault/runtime evidence; Support Envelope owns supported board/probe/host/docs truth; Release Truth owns shipped-image/imported-artifact truth. The next credible shared move is now split into an explicit proposal layer plus the ranked rollout: [`proposals/epic-firmware-productization-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-firmware-productization-stack.md) should own the stack-level contract, while [`design/device-lab-pilot-program.md`](./design/device-lab-pilot-program.md) should harden the physical-target lane first and [`design/firmware-productization-pilot-program.md`](./design/firmware-productization-pilot-program.md) should then widen from device truth into target/sysroot activation, footprint/layout evidence, runtime/fault evidence, and only after that support/release/incident consumers.

### Debugging + reliability
- **Tier 1:** Replay Kit (`cargo replay` + `replay-pack/v0`) — record/replay + minimize async/concurrency failures into shareable “bug cassettes”, integrating with tracing/Tokio Console and complementing lifecycle and deterministic-simulation lanes instead of pretending every observed failure is already replayable.

### Testing + service reliability
- **Tier 1:** DST Kit (`cargo dst` + `dst-pack/v0`) — standardize deterministic simulation seeds, fault models, histories, and CI review artifacts across Loom/Shuttle/Tokio/Turmoil/MadSim-style testing instead of forcing one simulator or pretending exploration and replay are the same thing.

### Async ecosystem + service reliability
- **Tier 0/1:** Lending Surface Kit (`cargo lending` + `lending-pack/v0`) — standardize borrow-friendly sequence surfaces, borrowing-mode profiles, async/runtime assumptions, adapter truth, and executable yield vectors across `Iterator`, streaming/lending iterators, `Stream`, nightly `AsyncIterator`, and future reborrow/generator lanes instead of leaving the next generation of sequence APIs to folklore, macros, or one more incompatible facade; treat `design/lending-surface-pilot-program.md` as the ranked rollout plan so sync lending, async bridges, borrowing callbacks, pin/runtime-sensitive adapters, and domain pilots stop getting discussed as if they were equally mature on day one.
- **Tier 0/1:** Async Lifecycle Kit (`async-core`, `async-scope`, `cargo asyncdoctor`) — portable task ownership, graceful shutdown, runtime-capability profiles, shutdown-policy artifacts, and lifecycle evidence for async services and libraries; treat `design/async-lifecycle-pilot-program.md` as the ranked rollout plan (Tokio service shutdown → runtime-portable library → supervised worker lanes → repro/debug consumers → constrained async) so this becomes a real review surface instead of another runtime wrapper.
- **Stack note:** treat **Async Lifecycle Kit + Replay Kit + DST Kit** as a shared **Async Reliability Stack**. Async Lifecycle owns task ownership, cancellation, shutdown policy, runtime-capability profiles, and lifecycle evidence; Replay owns concrete failure capture, minimization, and exact-vs-best-effort replay posture; DST owns seeds, world models, histories, and exploration reports. The stack now has an explicit proposal-layer candidate in [`proposals/epic-async-reliability-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-async-reliability-stack.md), and the next shared execution move remains the ranked rollout in [`design/async-reliability-pilot-program.md`](./design/async-reliability-pilot-program.md): Tokio shutdown truth first, then replay handoff, then deterministic-simulation attachment, then portability, and only after that widen to debugger / incident / support consumers.

### Dependency ergonomics + control
- **Tier 0/1:** Resolution Strategy Kit (`cargo resolve-plan` + `resolution-strategy-pack/v0`) — standardize named objective profiles, accepted-tradeoff reports, bounded candidate comparisons, and downstream handoff notes above chosen-graph evidence so reviewers can tell whether a lockfile or release targeted latest, MSRV fallback, direct-minimal validation, publish-time replay, or another declared strategy. Treat it as the objective/tradeoff layer above Dependency Control, not as a solver fork or policy engine.
- **Tier 0/1:** Resolution Doctor Kit (`cargo resolve` + `resolve-pack/v0`) — explain version choices, feature activation, duplicates, conflicts, and selection-policy posture in a portable form that other kits can consume.
