### Application/service interfaces + web backends
- **Tier 1/2:** Service Surface Kit (`cargo servicecheck` + `service-pack/v0`) — standardize declared service surfaces, stable route maps, middleware/runtime behavior profiles, checked exchange catalogs, and diffable service evidence across Rust web frameworks, `tower-http`, OpenAPI generators, and HTTP contract-test tooling rather than another web framework or codegen layer.

### RPC + transport protocols
- **Tier 1/2:** Protocol Surface Kit (`cargo protocolcheck` + `protocol-pack/v0`) — standardize declared wire-protocol families, transport/negotiation posture, interaction modes, security/ALPN/TLS assumptions, browser/native bridge lanes, and checked interoperability evidence across `tonic`, `tonic-web`, `h2`, Quinn, `h3`, WebSocket/WebTransport crates, `rustls`, and transport-agnostic RPC crates rather than another transport, proxy, or RPC framework.
- **Stack note:** treat **Protocol Surface Kit + Service Surface Kit + Runtime Settings Kit + Schema Contract Kit + Conformance Traceability Stack + Support Envelope Kit** as an explicit **Protocol Productization Stack**. Protocol Surface owns wire/transport/interaction/security/bridge truth; Service Surface owns application-facing service attachment plus health/reflection posture; Runtime Settings owns cert/root/ALPN/proxy/origin/feature activation; Schema Contract owns payload/interface evolution truth; Conformance Traceability owns spec/vector/interop evidence; Support Envelope and DocProof own public support/docs truth. The next credible shared move is the ranked rollout in [`design/protocol-productization-pilot-program.md`](./design/protocol-productization-pilot-program.md): gRPC + grpc-web truth → QUIC/HTTP3/WebTransport truth → transport-agnostic RPC truth → cross-language/conformance truth → support/docs/release consumers.

### Event-driven systems + messaging
- **Tier 1/2:** Event Surface Kit (`cargo eventcheck` + `event-pack/v0`) — standardize declared event surfaces, stable channel maps, envelope/schema attachments, delivery profiles, checked message-flow evidence, and diffable event-interface reports across AsyncAPI, CloudEvents, `async-nats`, `rdkafka`, `lapin`, `sea-streamer`, schema-registry helpers, and broker-backed test tooling rather than another broker client, schema registry, or queue abstraction.
- **Stack note:** treat **Event Surface Kit + Schema Contract Kit + Runtime Settings Kit + Observability Kit + Support Envelope Kit** as an explicit **Event Productization Stack**. Event Surface owns channel/event/envelope/delivery/replay truth; Schema Contract owns payload/compatibility and registry-subject truth; Runtime Settings owns broker/topology/durable/credential/TLS activation; Observability owns replay/DLQ/lag/correlation evidence; Support Envelope and DocProof own public support/docs truth. The next credible shared move is the ranked rollout in [`design/event-productization-pilot-program.md`](./design/event-productization-pilot-program.md): single-broker event lane → replay/DLQ/backfill lane → schema/registry lane → activation/observability lane → service/support/release/incident consumers.

### Background jobs + durable execution
- **Tier 1/2:** Background Work Kit (`cargo workcheck` + `work-pack/v0`) — standardize declared jobs/workflows, trigger maps, execution backends, retry/idempotency profiles, and checked execution evidence across `apalis`, `fang`, `sqlxmq`, cron schedulers, and durable workflow engines rather than another queue, cron crate, or orchestration runtime.
- **Stack note:** treat **Service Surface Kit + Runtime Settings Kit + Observability Kit + Async Reliability Stack + Background Work Kit + Support Envelope Kit** as a coupled **Service Productization Stack**. Service Surface owns route/middleware/exchange truth; Runtime Settings owns config/default/source/secret truth; Observability owns runtime evidence; Async Reliability owns lifecycle/replay posture; Background Work owns jobs/workflows; Support Envelope owns target/runtime/docs support truth. The next credible move is now explicit: a thin `cargo service product` / `service-product-pack/v0` layer sketched in [`proposals/epic-service-productization-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-service-productization-stack.md). See [`design/service-productization-stack.md`](./design/service-productization-stack.md) and [`design/service-productization-pilot-program.md`](./design/service-productization-pilot-program.md).
- **Stack note:** treat **Background Work Kit + Runtime Settings Kit + Observability Kit + Release Truth Stack + Support Envelope Kit** as an explicit **Workflow Productization Stack**. Background Work owns declared jobs/workflows/triggers/schedules/retry/idempotency truth; Runtime Settings owns engine/store/retention activation; Observability owns journal/history/retry/recovery evidence; Release Truth owns worker/runtime/source lineage for long-lived executions; Support Envelope and DocProof own public support/docs truth. The next credible shared move is the ranked rollout in [`design/workflow-productization-pilot-program.md`](./design/workflow-productization-pilot-program.md), now backed by [`proposals/epic-workflow-productization-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-workflow-productization-stack.md): a thin `cargo workflow-product` / `workflow-product-pack/v0` layer that keeps declared work truth, activation truth, history truth, release lineage, and downstream consumer conclusions separate rather than flattening Rust durable work into another queue/runtime/framework shootout.

### Failure interfaces + operator UX
- **Tier 1/2:** Diagnostic Surface Kit (`cargo diagnose` + `diagnostic-pack/v0`) — standardize declared diagnostic codes, help/docs metadata, redaction policy, surface-specific renderings, and checked failure evidence across `thiserror`, `anyhow`, `miette`, `error-stack`, `tracing-error`, path-aware deserialization diagnostics, and HTTP Problem Details crates rather than another error type or reporter.

### CLI applications + command UX
- **Tier 1/2:** Command Surface Kit (`cargo cmdcheck` + `command-pack/v0`) — standardize declared command surfaces, generated help/completions/manpages, transcript/example catalogs, and shell/platform check evidence across `clap`, `clap_complete`, `clap_mangen`, `assert_cmd`, `trycmd`, `snapbox`, and Cargo subcommands rather than another parser or snapshot runner.

### Terminal UX + TUI behavior
- **Tier 1/2:** Terminal Surface Kit (`cargo termsurf` + `terminal-pack/v0`) — standardize declared terminal capabilities, render/input posture, Unicode-layout policy, graphics-protocol assumptions, cleanup/restore guarantees, and checked terminal evidence across `crossterm`, Ratatui backends, `termwiz`, `anstream`, `reedline`, `vt100`, and richer terminal-protocol adapters rather than another backend facade, style crate, or screenshot-only TUI story. Treat `design/terminal-surface-lane-map.md` and `design/terminal-surface-pilot-program.md` as the next credible refinement so styled-output/color-policy, full-screen TUI, line-editor, capability/probing, parser/test, and graphics lanes stay explicit.

### Learning + documentation reliability
- **Tier 0/1:** DocProof Kit (`cargo docproof` + `doc-pack/v0`) — standardize guide/example/doc-host support envelopes, executable-doc validation plans, and attachable doc evidence across rustdoc doctests, mdBook, docs.rs builds, CLI transcript tests, and compile-fail teaching flows so docs become reviewable support claims rather than best-effort prose. Treat [`design/docproof-pilot-program.md`](./design/docproof-pilot-program.md) as the ranked rollout plan (API docs + docs.rs → CLI transcripts → guide books → compile-fail teaching → release/support consumers) so documentation truth becomes portable before atlas/release/support consumers widen.
- **Stack note:** treat **DocProof Kit + Compile Guidance Kit** as a coupled **Canonical Learning Stack**. DocProof owns guide/example/hosted-doc truth; Compile Guidance owns compile-time diagnostics, lint catalogs, and compile-fail guidance semantics; Semantic Context / Edit Workflow / Atlas / assistant consumers should import these as canonical inputs rather than silently replacing them. The next shared execution move is the explicit consumer-import layer in [`design/canonical-learning-consumer-pilot-program.md`](./design/canonical-learning-consumer-pilot-program.md), which should start with docs-host imports and only later widen to editors, assistants, atlas, and review consumers.

### Safety evidence + verification
- **Tier 0/1:** Safety Evidence Kit (`cargo safety` + `safety-pack/v0`) — standardize reviewable safety cases with unsafe-surface inventory, normative-contract traceability, safety-lint posture, criterion-aware coverage evidence, verification claims, and explicit waivers across docs/contracts/Clippy/coverage/sanitizers/proof tools instead of bespoke certification binders and CI folklore.
- **Tier 0/1:** Formal Verification Kit (`cargo verify` + `verify-pack/v0`) — standardize verification intent, backend capability declarations, proof/counterexample reports, and diffable proof artifacts across Kani, Creusot, Prusti, Verus, ESBMC/Flux/VeriFast-adjacent flows, and future backends without flattening their differences. Treat [`design/formal-verification-pilot-program.md`](./design/formal-verification-pilot-program.md) as the ranked rollout plan (Cargo-native pair across unlike families → replay/counterexample lane → std-contract/library slice → multi-backend diff → safety-critical import) so proof/report interoperability becomes real before assurance consumers widen the claim.
- **Stack note:** treat **Safety Evidence Kit + Coverage Evidence Kit + Sanitizer Battery Kit + Formal Verification Kit** as a shared **Safety-Critical Evidence Stack**. Safety Evidence owns the reviewable case summary; Coverage Evidence owns explicit criterion semantics; Sanitizer Battery owns runtime-checking lane truth; Formal Verification owns proof intent, assumptions, backend-family differences, and counterexamples. The next shared execution moves are now explicit: first harden criterion-aware coverage through [`design/coverage-evidence-pilot-program.md`](./design/coverage-evidence-pilot-program.md), harden proof/report interoperability through [`design/formal-verification-pilot-program.md`](./design/formal-verification-pilot-program.md), and only then widen through the ranked rollout in [`design/safety-critical-pilot-program.md`](./design/safety-critical-pilot-program.md) plus the thin composition layer sketched in [`proposals/epic-safety-critical-evidence-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-safety-critical-evidence-stack.md).

### Dynamic analysis + exploit mitigations
- **Tier 0/1:** Sanitizer Battery Kit (`cargo sanitize` + `sanitize-pack/v0`) — standardize declared dynamic-analysis lanes, instrumented-runtime provenance, execution-import truth, capability profiles, observation/finding reports, and waiver-aware diffable packs across Miri, `cargo-careful`, LLVM sanitizers, exploit-mitigation lanes, and future BorrowSanitizer-style engines instead of flattening every runtime check into one fake “sanitizer pass”. Treat [`design/sanitizer-battery-lane-map.md`](./design/sanitizer-battery-lane-map.md) as the normative lane split and [`design/sanitizer-battery-pilot-program.md`](./design/sanitizer-battery-pilot-program.md) as the ranked rollout plan (Miri isolated baseline → Miri + runner import → `cargo-careful` native checking lane → LLVM sanitizer + instrumented std finding lane → mitigation/hardening lane → Borrow lane → safety-critical import) so lane and runner honesty harden before assurance consumers widen the claim.

### Higher-level Rust / scripting
- **Tier 0/1:** ScriptKit (`cargo scriptkit` + `script-pack/v0`) — make Cargo single-file packages reviewable with explicit subject identity, frontmatter/default truth, lock/cache lane reports, discovery/workspace posture, consumer-import reports, and portable packs. Treat [`design/scriptkit-pilot-program.md`](./design/scriptkit-pilot-program.md) as the ranked rollout plan (minimal bug repro → shebang utility → in-repo automation → editor/CI import → workspace opt-in/policy) so one-file Rust becomes a governed execution lane rather than a temp-project folklore path or unreviewed repo side door.

### Tooling + IDE cohesion
- **Tier 0/1:** Edit Workflow Kit (`cargo editflow` + `edit-pack/v0`) — standardize candidate-edit capture, conflict-aware selection plans, application reports, and verification packs across `rustc` suggestions, `cargo fix`, edition migrations, rust-analyzer assists / SSR, and future assistant or CI-generated refactors instead of hiding edit governance inside whichever tool happened to mutate the working tree. Treat it as the governed-execution counterpart to Semantic Context Kit rather than a standalone autofix gadget; the next credible move is a ranked pilot program (compiler suggestions → Cargo fix / edition waves → rust-analyzer assist export → assistant proposals under manual review → CI consumers), not direct assistant mutation or another opaque bulk-fix tool.
- **Tier 1:** CompileDB Kit (`cargo compiledb` + `compiledb-pack/v0`) — generate deterministic compile_commands.json for native code built via build.rs/cc, align with Rust targets, and ship shareable packs for IDEs/CI.

### Data + persistence workflows
- **Tier 1:** Database Contract Kit (`cargo dbcheck` + `db-pack/v0`) — standardize live database intent, schema snapshots, query-validation evidence, migration execution reports, runtime-activation attachments, release/support-ready imports, and test-environment contracts across SQLx, Diesel, SeaORM, refinery, and Testcontainers rather than another ORM, migration runner, or hosted data-control plane.
- **Tier 1/2:** Dataset Surface Kit (`cargo datasetcheck` + `dataset-pack/v0`) — standardize declared dataset/table surfaces, layout profiles, engine-compatibility evidence, checked samples, and diffable data-interface reports across Arrow, Polars, DataFusion, Parquet, `object_store`, Delta Lake, and Iceberg rather than another dataframe engine, query engine, or lakehouse format.

Data Productization Stack = **Database Contract Kit + Schema Contract Kit + Migration Truth Stack + Runtime Settings Kit + Support Envelope Kit**. Ownership rule: Database Contract owns live relational truth, Schema Contract owns public service/data schemas, Migration Truth owns source→destination rollout choreography, Runtime Settings owns activation truth, and Support Envelope/DocProof own what is actually promised. The shared execution move is `design/data-productization-pilot-program.md`, and the explicit proposal-layer candidate is now [`proposals/epic-data-productization-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-data-productization-stack.md): a thin `cargo data-product` / `data-product-pack/v0` layer above those lanes, with query/offline truth ranked first and downstream release/support/incident consumers later.


### Cluster operators + control-plane workflows
Operator Productization Stack = **Schema Contract Kit + Runtime Capability Kit + Service Surface Kit + Observability Kit + Distribution Contract Stack + Support Envelope Kit**. Ownership rule: Schema Contract owns CRD/version/spec/status/condition truth, Runtime Capability owns RBAC/resources/external-power posture, Service Surface owns webhook/health/admin endpoint behavior, Observability owns reconcile/status/event/metric evidence, and Distribution Contract + Support Envelope/DocProof own install/upgrade/Kubernetes-version/runtime/docs truth. Policy, Conformance Traceability, Release Truth, and Incident lanes should remain importing consumers rather than silently becoming the owner of operator-product truth. The shared execution move is `design/operator-productization-pilot-program.md`, and the explicit proposal-layer candidate is [`proposals/epic-operator-productization-stack.md`](./proposals/epic-operator-productization-stack.md): a thin `cargo operator-product` / `operator-product-pack/v0` layer that keeps CRD/schema truth, capability posture, webhook surfaces, runtime evidence, install/support truth, and downstream conclusions separate while letting real Rust operator products compose them honestly.

Geospatial Productization Stack = **Geospatial Surface Kit + Dataset Surface Kit + Runtime Settings Kit + Support Envelope Kit**. - **Tier 1/2:** Robot Description Surface Kit (`cargo robotdesc` + `robot-description-pack/v0`) — standardize declared robot subjects, structure/frame topology, geometry/assets, limits/calibration posture, and checked/runtime-attached description evidence across `urdf-rs`, `robot-description-builder`, `k`, `urdf-viz`, Rerun, ROS 2 Rust runtime lanes, and replay/import tooling rather than another robotics framework, simulator, parser-only crate, or transport winner pitch.

- **Tier 1/2:** Model Productization Stack (`model-pack/v0` imports + support/activation evidence) — standardize how model identity, tokenizer behavior, artifact variants, backend/device/precision posture, hub/cache/auth activation, and checked support/docs claims compose across Candle, Burn, `ort`, `tract`, `tokenizers`, `hf_hub`, safetensors, GGUF, and Hub metadata rather than another runtime bake-off, file-format winner, or model-serving control plane.

Search Productization Stack = **Retrieval Surface Kit + Dataset Surface Kit + Runtime Settings Kit + Observability Kit + Support Envelope Kit**. ### Data contracts + service boundaries
- **Tier 1:** Schema Contract Kit (`cargo schema` + `schema-pack/v0`) — export/diff/validate Serde, JSON Schema, OpenAPI, and Protobuf contracts with compatibility reason codes and optional migration attachments.

### Testing + assurance
- **Tier 1:** FuzzPack Kit (`cargo fuzzpack` + `fuzz-pack/v0`) — standardize fuzz target profiles, portable corpus/crash/reproducer packs, persisted property-test regression imports, shrink/minimization traces, campaign diffs, and execution-envelope truth across `cargo-fuzz`, `honggfuzz`, `test-fuzz`, `proptest`, and richer engine imports instead of another engine-specific helper or CI-only artifact pile.

### Library evolution + compatibility
- **Tier 0/1:** Public API Kit (`cargo api` + `api-pack/v0`) — standardize one release-admission boundary for public API surface export, cross-crate provenance, declared-vs-inferred public-dependency exposure, semver reason codes, witness-based type-compatibility evidence, and MSRV verification around `cargo publish` / CI / downstream review. The next credible move should follow a ranked pilot program (single-crate release gate → workspace release group → downstream packaging intake → policy correlation → build/change-impact consumers) rather than adding more bespoke semver outputs. (High leverage because it ties library evolution directly into Cargo’s supply-chain and publish workflows.)
Library Productization Stack = **Public API Kit + Manifest Truth Stack + Release Truth Stack + Support Envelope Kit + DocProof Kit**. Ownership rule: Public API owns exposed Rust contract / semver / MSRV / public-dependency truth; Manifest Truth owns feature/default/package/docs.rs/native/build-script posture; Release Truth owns what source package and provenance were actually published; Support Envelope and DocProof own target/docs/example/support truth; Trust / Policy / Inventory remain downstream imports rather than silent owners. The shared execution move is `design/library-productization-pilot-program.md`, which ranks a single library release lane first, docs.rs + feature/default posture second, public-dependency + native/build-script posture third, support/release drift fourth, and downstream consumer imports last.

### Change management + upgrades
- **Tier 0/1:** Migration Kit (`cargo migrate` + `migration-pack/v0`) — standardize explicit migration subjects, source→destination intent, configuration-scope analysis, staged execution plans, and attachable run/outcome evidence across edition upgrades, cargo-fix-era edit flows, toolchain/MSRV ratchets, dependency upgrades, and support/API-sensitive transitions so upgrades stop living in issue comments, CI folklore, and maintainer memory. Treat it as the orchestration layer of the broader **Migration Truth Stack** with Edit Workflow, Public API, Support Envelope, DocProof, and Downstream Testing; the stack-level direction is now explicit in `proposals/epic-migration-truth-stack.md`, and the next credible move is the ranked rollout in `design/migration-pilot-program.md` (edition transition → workspace toolchain / `rust-version` → dependency + API/MSRV → docs/support/downstream → release/archaeology).


### Macro authoring + compile-time ergonomics
- **Tier 1:** Macro Workflow Kit (`cargo macro` + `macro-pack/v0`) — standardize proc-macro inventory, expansion packs, cost/debug reports, and migration hints so teams can review today's macro-heavy workflows and plan toward future declarative/reflection alternatives without pretending one tool owns the whole problem; treat it as the workflow/migration pillar of the broader **Compile-Time Surface Stack** and the current-burden pillar of the narrower **Reflection Transition Stack**, including evidence for when a subject should stay `narrow-native`, aim for `portable-sandbox`, or target `language-first` migration.
